2.5pt\hbox{$$}}$}\hss}}\kern 1.43518pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{$\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$A,{A}^{\bot}$}\kern 0.0pt$}}}}}\kern 0.0pt}\kern 10.00002pt}\hbox{\hbox{$\cdots$}}\kern 10.00002pt}\hbox{\kern 9.36938pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\kern 12.26392pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{$$}}}\kern 12.26392pt}\kern 1.43518pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\smash{\lower 2.5pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{ax}\;$}}$}}\vbox{\vbox to0.4pt{\vfill\hbox to24.52783pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt}\vfill}}\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\smash{\lower 2.5pt\hbox{$$}}$}\hss}}\kern 1.43518pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{$\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$A,{A}^{\bot}$}\kern 0.0pt$}}}}}\kern 0.0pt}\kern 10.00002pt}\hbox{\hbox{$\cdots$}}}}}\kern 1.43518pt\hbox{\kern 9.36938pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\smash{\lower 2.5pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{multicut}\;$}}$}}\vbox{\vbox to0.4pt{\vfill\hbox to103.4251pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt}\vfill}}\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\smash{\lower 2.5pt\hbox{$$}}$}\hss}\kern 0.0pt}\kern 1.43518pt\hbox{\kern 57.33192pt\hbox{\hbox{$A$}}\kern 47.96254pt}}}\kern 0.0pt}}}

The most interesting aspect of our technique is that it allows us to focus on the study of the dynamics of the bottom-most
\mathsf{cut}
-rule in a pile of
\mathsf{cut}
-rules (corresponding to a multicut) to deduce important properties of the limit coderivations.
This is possible because in progressive derivations there cannot be infinite piles of
\mathsf{cut}
-rules, therefore a cut-elimination step is eventually applied to a rule on top of a pile of
\mathsf{cut}
-rules, which therefore is progressively pushed by the height-by-height
\mathsf{mc}\text{-}\mathsf{ices}
below the pile, or simply removed by a cut-elimination step involving one of the
\mathsf{cut}
-rules of the pile.
This interesting dynamic is hidden by multicut-elimination, which lacks the granularity required to observe it.

5.4
Continuous Cut-elimination preserves (weak-)regularity

To prove that continuous cut-elimination preserves regularity and weak-regularity, we define the notion of
depth
of a coderivation, akin in linear logic, as the maximal number of nested
nwb
s.

Definition 42
.

Let
\mathcal{D}\in\mathsf{PLL}^{\!\infty}
.
The
nesting level of a sequent occurrence

\Gamma
in
\mathcal{D}

is the number
\mathbf{nl}_{\mathcal{D}}({\Gamma})

of nodes below it that are the root of a call of a
nwb
.
The
nesting level of a formula (occurrence)

A
in
\mathcal{D}
, noted
\mathbf{nl}_{\mathcal{D}}({A})
, is the nesting level of the sequent that contain that formula.
The
nesting level of a rule

\mathsf{r}
in
\mathcal{D}
, noted
\mathbf{nl}_{\mathcal{D}}({\mathsf{r}})

(resp.
of a sub-coderivation

\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
of
\mathcal{D}
, noted
\mathbf{nl}_{\mathcal{D}}({\mathcal{D}^{\prime}})
),
is the nesting level of the conclusion of
\mathsf{r}

(resp. conclusion of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
).

The
depth of
\mathcal{D}

is

\mathbf{d}(\mathcal{D})\coloneqq\sup_{\mathsf{r}\in\mathcal{D}}\{\mathbf{nl}_{\mathcal{D}}({\mathsf{r}})\}\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
.

Remark 43
.

All calls of a
nwb
have the same nesting level.
Moreover, each of the sequents of its main branch have nesting level
0
.

Cut-elimination
\to_{\mathsf{cut}}
on
\mathsf{PLL}^{\!\infty}
enjoys the following property.

Lemma 44
.

Let
\mathcal{D},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\in\mathsf{PLL}^{\!\infty}
.
If
\mathcal{D}\to_{\mathsf{cut}}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
then
\mathbf{d}(\mathcal{D})\geq\mathbf{d}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime})
.

Proof.

By inspection of the cut-elimination steps in
Figures

3
,
5
and
10
.
∎

Lemma 45
.

If
\mathcal{D}
is weakly regular then
\mathbf{d}(\mathcal{D})\in\mathbb{N}
.

Proof.

Since
\mathcal{D}
is weakly regular, it has only finitely many distinct sub-coderivations whose conclusion is the left premise of a
\mathsf{c\mathsf{\oc p}}
rule. Therefore,
\mathbf{d}(\mathcal{D})\in\mathbb{N}
.
∎

Proposition 46
.

If
\mathcal{D}
is progressing and finitely expandable then so is
f_{\sigma^{\infty}}(\mathcal{D})
.

Proof.

By
Theorem

40
,
f_{\sigma^{\infty}}(\mathcal{D})
is cut-free and progressing. To conclude, we need to show that any infinite branch
\mathcal{B}
of
f_{\sigma^{\infty}}(\mathcal{D})
has only finitely many
\wn\mathsf{b}
rules. To this end, we notice that the cut-elimination step
\mathsf{c\mathsf{\oc p}}\mbox{-vs-}\wn\mathsf{b}
erases a
\wn\mathsf{b}
rule
\mathsf{r}
and creates a finite sequence of
\wn\mathsf{b}
rules in the same branch of
\mathsf{r}
. No other cut-elimination step creates
\wn\mathsf{b}
rules.
This means that, if
\mathcal{B}
had infinitely many
\wn\mathsf{b}
rules, either a branch containing infinite
\wn\mathsf{b}
rules exists already in
\mathcal{D}
, or
\mathcal{D}
contains a branch with infinitely many
\mathsf{c\mathsf{\oc p}}
rules whose principal
\oc
-formula will be eventually active for a cut of the form
\mathsf{c\mathsf{\oc p}}\mbox{-vs-}\wn\mathsf{b}
. Both cases are impossible by progressiveness of
\mathcal{D}
.
∎

Proposition 47
.

Let
\mathcal{D}\in\mathsf{wr}\mathsf{PLL}^{\!\infty}
(resp.
\mathsf{r}\mathsf{PLL}^{\!\infty}
).
Then
f_{\sigma^{\infty}}(\mathcal{D})

admits a decomposition prebar, and

\mathsf{base}(f_{\sigma^{\infty}}(\mathcal{D}))=\mathsf{base}(\sigma^{\infty}_{\mathcal{D}}(n))
for some
n\geq 0
.

Proof.

By
Theorem

40
and
Proposition

46
,

f_{\sigma^{\infty}}(\mathcal{D})
is a
\mathsf{hyp}
-free,
\mathsf{cut}
-free, progressing
and finitely expandable coderivation.
By
Proposition

27

f_{\sigma^{\infty}}(\mathcal{D})
admits a decomposition prebar
\mathsf{border}(\mathcal{D})=\{v_{1},\ldots,v_{k}\}
.
By continuity of
f_{\sigma^{\infty}}
, this means that there is
n\geq 0
such that
\mathsf{base}(\sigma^{\infty}_{\mathcal{D}}(n))=\mathsf{base}(f_{\sigma^{\infty}}(\mathcal{D}))
.
Note that
\mathsf{base}(\sigma^{\infty}_{\mathcal{D}}(n))
exists by
Propositions

22
and
27
.
∎

Theorem 48
.

Continuous cut-elimination preserves (weak-)regularity.
That is, if
\mathcal{D}\in\mathsf{wr}\mathsf{PLL}^{\!\infty}
(resp.
\mathcal{D}\in\mathsf{r}\mathsf{PLL}^{\!\infty}
), then so is
f_{\sigma^{\infty}}(\mathcal{D})
.

Proof.

To prove the statement we
define a maximal and
transfinite

\mathsf{ices}

\sigma=\{\sigma_{\mathcal{D}}\}_{\mathcal{D}\in\mathsf{o}\mathsf{PLL}^{\!\infty}}
eliminating the cut and preserving (weak) regularity, finite expandability and (weak) progressivity.
Then show that we can define a
\mathsf{mc}\text{-}\mathsf{ices}

\sigma^{*}=\{\sigma^{*}_{\mathcal{D}}\}_{\mathcal{D}\in\mathsf{o}\mathsf{PLL}^{\!\infty}}
by “compressing”
\sigma
, using a technique similar to the one in
[
32
]
.
This allows us to conclude since, by
Proposition

30
, we have that
f_{\sigma^{\infty}}=f_{\sigma^{*}}
.
In particular, every transfinite
\mathsf{ices}
that we will construct will satisfy the following conditions for any limit ordinal
\lambda\leq\mathsf{length}(\sigma_{\mathcal{D}})
:

1.

\sigma^{\infty}_{\mathcal{D}}(\lambda)=\bigsqcup_{i<\lambda}\tilde{\mathcal{D}}_{i}
for some
\tilde{\mathcal{D}}_{i}
finite approximations of
\sigma^{\infty}_{\mathcal{D}}(i)
,

2.

if
h_{i}
is the height of the
\mathsf{cut}
-rule reduced at the
i
-th step of
\sigma^{\infty}_{\mathcal{D}}
, then
\mathsf{lim}_{i<\lambda}{\left({h_{i}}\right)}=\infty
.

By
Lemma

45
we know that
d=\mathbf{d}(\mathcal{D})
is finite.
Then we define
\sigma_{\mathcal{D}}
as follows:

•

If
d=0
, then by
Proposition

27

\mathcal{D}
is a derivation
and there is a maximal (finite) cut-elimination sequence
\sigma_{\mathcal{D}}

rewriting
\mathcal{D}
to a
\mathsf{cut}
-free and
\mathsf{hyp}
-free derivation (see
Lemma

26
).

•

If
d>0
,
then
we know by
Proposition

47
that there is

n_{\mathsf{base}(\mathcal{D})}\geq 0
and a finite sequence
\sigma^{\mathsf{base}}

such that

\sigma^{\mathsf{base}}(0)=\mathcal{D}

and

\mathsf{base}(f_{\sigma^{\infty}}(\mathcal{D}))=\mathsf{base}(\sigma^{\mathsf{base}}(n_{\mathsf{base}(\mathcal{D})}))

where

20.00003pt}}}\kern 0.0pt}}}\kern 0.0pt\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 7.9031pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$$}}\vbox{}\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$$}\hss}\kern 7.9031pt}\hbox{\kern to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$$}}\vbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 20.00003pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 2.5pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{cut}\;$}}$}}\vbox{\vbox to0.4pt{\vfill\hbox to86.76712pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt}\vfill}}\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\smash{\lower 2.5pt\hbox{$$}}$}\hss}\kern 0.0pt}\kern 1.43518pt\hbox{\kern 37.81848pt\hbox{\hbox{$\Delta_{i},A_{i}$}}\kern 29.91537pt}}}\kern 0.0pt}\kern 10.00002pt}\hbox{\kern 11.7694pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\kern 17.21819pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{$\vdots$}}}\kern 17.21819pt}\kern 1.43518pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\smash{\lower 2.5pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c\mathsf{\oc p}}\;$}}$}}\vbox{\vbox to0.4pt{\vfill\hbox to34.43637pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt}\vfill}}\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\smash{\lower 2.5pt\hbox{$$}}$}\hss}}\kern 1.43518pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{$\kern 0.0pt\hbox{${\wn\Delta_{i},\oc A_{i}}$}\kern 0.0pt$}}}}}\kern 0.0pt}}}}\kern 1.43518pt\hbox{\kern 41.94864pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\smash{\lower 2.5pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c\mathsf{\oc p}}\;$}}$}}\vbox{\vbox to0.4pt{\vfill\hbox to113.05751pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt}\vfill}}\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\smash{\lower 2.5pt\hbox{$$}}$}\hss}\kern 0.0pt}\kern 1.43518pt\hbox{\kern 94.72739pt\hbox{\hbox{$\reflectbox{$\ddots$}$}}\kern 52.77875pt}}}\kern 0.0pt}}}}\kern 1.43518pt\hbox{\kern 41.94864pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\smash{\lower 2.5pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c\mathsf{\oc p}}\;$}}$}}\vbox{\vbox to0.4pt{\vfill\hbox to169.07913pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt}\vfill}}\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\smash{\lower 2.5pt\hbox{$$}}$}\hss}\kern 52.77875pt}\kern 1.43518pt\hbox{\kern 109.27002pt\hbox{\hbox{${\wn\Delta_{i},\oc A_{i}}$}}\kern 120.10013pt}}}\kern 0.0pt}}}}\kern 1.43518pt\hbox{\kern 41.94864pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\smash{\lower 2.5pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c\mathsf{\oc p}}\;$}}$}}\vbox{\vbox to0.4pt{\vfill\hbox to210.55814pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt}\vfill}}\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\smash{\lower 2.5pt\hbox{$$}}$}\hss}\kern 120.10013pt}\kern 1.43518pt\hbox{\kern 130.00952pt\hbox{\hbox{$\wn\Delta_{i},\oc A_{i}$}}\kern 208.16101pt}}}\kern 0.0pt}}}

Note that, since
\mathcal{D}
is (weakly) regular, then each
{\mathcal{D}^{\textsf{nwb}}_{i}}
is also (weakly) regular.
By induction hypothesis, each call of the
nwb
s

\mathcal{D}^{\textsf{nwb}}_{i}
with
i\in\{1,\ldots,m\}

has strictly smaller depth.
Therefore for all
i\in\{1,\ldots,m\}
we can define a cut-elimination sequence
\sigma_{i}^{\textsf{nwb}}
such that
\sigma_{i}^{\textsf{nwb}}(0)=\mathcal{D}^{\textsf{nwb}}_{i}
that satisfies conditions
1
and
2
, eliminates the cut and preserves (weak) regularity, finite expandability and (weak) progressiveness.

We define the sequence
\sigma_{\mathcal{D}}
with transfinite length which
first performs all cut-elimination steps required to remove reducible
\mathsf{cut}
-rule in
\lfloor{\mathcal{D}}\rfloor_{\mathsf{border}(\mathcal{D})}
,
then performs all
\mathsf{c\mathsf{\oc p}}\mbox{-vs-}\mathsf{c\mathsf{\oc p}}
steps,
and finally applies cut-elimination steps to each call of the resulting new
\textsf{nwb}s
created by merging the calls. That is,

\sigma_{\mathcal{D}}=\sigma^{\mathsf{base}}\cdot\sigma^{\prime}\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{m}\sigma^{\textsf{nwb}}_{i}

where the product of sequences denotes their concatenation.
By construction
\sigma_{\mathcal{D}}
satisfies conditions
1
and
2
, eliminates the cut and preserves (weak) regularity, finite expandability and (weak) progressiveness.

It is clear that
\sigma\coloneqq\{\sigma_{\mathcal{D}}\}_{\mathcal{D}\in\mathsf{o}\mathsf{PLL}^{\!\infty}}
is a maximal transfinite
\mathsf{ices}
. To conclude we have to define a
\mathsf{mc}\text{-}\mathsf{ices}

\sigma^{*}=\{\sigma^{*}_{\mathcal{D}}\}_{\mathcal{D}\in\mathsf{o}\mathsf{PLL}^{\!\infty}}
containing only sequences of length at most
\omega
such that
f_{\sigma^{*}}(\mathcal{D})
is the same coderivation of the limit of
\sigma_{\mathcal{D}}
.
We construct each
\sigma^{*}_{\mathcal{D}}
by induction on
d=\mathbf{d}(\mathcal{D})
.

•

If
d=0
, then
\sigma_{\mathcal{D}}
is finite and
\sigma^{*}_{\mathcal{D}}\coloneqq\sigma_{\mathcal{D}}
.

•

Otherwise
d>0
.
By induction hypothesis, for all
i\in\{1,\ldots,m\}
, we can construct from each
\sigma_{i}^{\textsf{nwb}}
a sequence
\sigma_{i}^{*}
with
\mathsf{length}(\sigma^{*}_{i})\leq\omega
such that
\sigma_{i}^{*}(0)=\mathcal{D}^{\textsf{nwb}}_{i}
and
having the same limit, i.e.,
\sigma^{*}_{i}(\mathsf{length}(\sigma^{*}_{i}))=\hat{\sigma}_{\mathcal{D}}(\mathsf{length}{(\sigma_{i}^{\textsf{nwb}})})
. This means that the following cut-elimination sequence

\hat{\sigma}_{\mathcal{D}}\coloneqq\sigma^{\mathsf{base}}\cdot\sigma^{\prime}\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{m}\sigma^{*}_{i}

has the same limit as
\sigma_{\mathcal{D}}
and satisfies conditions
1
and
2
. We notice that any cut-elimination step in
\sigma^{*}_{i}
commutes with any cut-elimination step in
\sigma^{*}_{j}
for any
i,j\in\{1,\ldots,m\}
. Moreover, any cut-elimination step in
\sigma^{*}_{i}
with
i\in\{k+1,\ldots,m\}
commutes with any cut-elimination step in
\sigma^{\prime}
. Finally, for any cut-elimination step in
\sigma^{*}_{i}
with
i\in\{1,\ldots,k\}
there is a
j_{0}\geq 0
such that it commutes with the
j
-th cut-elimination step in
\sigma^{\prime}
for any
j\geq j_{0}
. This means that we can define
\sigma_{\mathcal{D}}^{*}
by reordering the cut-elimination steps from
\hat{\sigma}_{\mathcal{D}}
in such a way that we alternate one step in
\sigma^{\prime}
with
m
steps from each
\sigma^{*}_{i}
. Clearly,
\sigma_{\mathcal{D}}^{*}
is such that
\mathsf{length}(\sigma_{\mathcal{D}}^{*})\leq\omega
, and it satisfies conditions
1
and
2
. Moreover, since
\hat{\sigma}_{\mathcal{D}}
satisfies condition
2
, it is easy to show that this reordering preserves the limit, i.e.,
\sigma^{*}_{\mathcal{D}}(\mathsf{length}(\sigma^{*}_{\mathcal{D}}))=\hat{\sigma}_{\mathcal{D}}(\mathsf{length}{(\hat{\sigma}_{\mathcal{D}})})
. Finally, since
\sigma_{\mathcal{D}}^{*}
satisfies condition
1
, the
\mathsf{ices}

\sigma^{*}\coloneqq\{\sigma^{*}_{\mathcal{D}}\}_{\mathcal{D}\in\mathsf{o}\mathsf{PLL}^{\!\infty}}
is a (maximal and) continuous
\mathsf{ices}
, i.e.,
f_{\sigma^{*}}
is Scott-continuous. Therefore,
\sigma^{*}
is a
\mathsf{mc}\text{-}\mathsf{ices}
and, by
Proposition

30
, we have:

\hat{\sigma}_{\mathcal{D}}(\mathsf{length}{(\hat{\sigma}_{\mathcal{D}})})=f_{\sigma^{*}}(\mathcal{D})=f_{\sigma^{\infty}}(\mathcal{D})

which implies that
f_{\sigma^{\infty}}(\mathcal{D})
is (weakly) regular, finitely expandable and (weakly) progressive.

∎

6
Relational semantics for non-wellfounded proofs

Here we define a denotational model for
\mathsf{o}\mathsf{PLL}^{\!\infty}
based on
relational semantics
, which interprets an open coderivation as the union of the interpretations of its finite approximations, as in
[
17
]
.
We show that relational semantics is sound for
\mathsf{o}\mathsf{PLL}^{\!\infty}
, but not for its extension with digging.

Relational semantics interprets exponential by finite multisets, denoted by brackets, e.g.,
[x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}]
;

+
denotes the
multiset union
, and

\mathcal{M}_{f}({X})
denotes the set of finite multisets over a set
X
.
To correctly define the semantics of a coderivation, we need to see sequents as
finite sequences
of formulas (taking their order into account), which means that we have to add an
exchange
rule to
\mathsf{o}\mathsf{PLL}^{\!\infty}
to swap the order of two consecutive formulas in a sequent.

Definition 49
.

We
associate with each formula
A
a
set

\llbracket A\rrbracket
defined as follows:

\llbracket X\rrbracket\coloneqq D_{X}\quad\llbracket\mathsf{1}\rrbracket\coloneqq\{*\}\quad\llbracket A\mathop{\otimes}B\rrbracket\coloneqq\llbracket A\rrbracket\times\llbracket B\rrbracket\quad\llbracket\oc A\rrbracket\coloneqq\mathcal{M}_{f}({\llbracket A\rrbracket})\quad\llbracket{A}^{\bot}\rrbracket\coloneqq\llbracket A\rrbracket

where
D_{X}
is an arbitrary set.
For a sequent
\Gamma=A_{1},\ldots,A_{n}
, we set

\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket\coloneqq\llbracket A_{1}\invamp\cdots\invamp A_{n}\rrbracket
.

Given
\mathcal{D}\in\mathsf{PLL}\cup\mathsf{o}\mathsf{PLL}^{\!\infty}
with conclusion
\Gamma
,
we set
\llbracket\mathcal{D}\rrbracket\coloneqq\bigcup_{n\geq 0}\llbracket\mathcal{D}\rrbracket_{n}\subseteq\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket
,
where
\llbracket\mathcal{D}\rrbracket_{0}=\varnothing

and, for all
i\in\mathbb{N}\setminus\{0\}
,

\llbracket\mathcal{D}\rrbracket_{i}
is defined inductively according to
Figure

12
.

\begin{array}[]{l}\left\llbracket{\vbox{\hbox{\kern 9.36938pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\kern 12.26392pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{$$}}}\kern 12.26392pt}\kern 1.43518pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\smash{\lower Inductive definition of the set
\left\llbracket\mathcal{D}\right\rrbracket_{n}
, for
n>0
.

Example 50
.

For the coderivations
\mathcal{D}_{\!\lightning}
and
\mathcal{D}_{\wn}
in
Figure

7
,

\llbracket\mathcal{D}_{\!\lightning}\rrbracket=\llbracket\mathcal{D}_{\wn}\rrbracket=\varnothing
.
For the derivations
\underline{0}
and
\underline{1}
in
Figure

2
,
\llbracket\underline{0}\rrbracket=\{([~],(x,x))\mid x\in D_{X}\}
and
\llbracket\underline{1}\rrbracket=\{([(x,y)],(x,y))\mid x,y\in D_{X}\}
.
For the coderivation
\mathsf{c\mathsf{\oc p}}_{(\underline{i_{0}},\ldots,\underline{i_{n}},\ldots)}
in
Example

10
(with
i_{j}\in\{0,1\}
for all
j\in\mathbb{N}
),

\llbracket\mathsf{c\mathsf{\oc p}}_{(\underline{i_{0}},\ldots,\underline{i_{n}},\ldots)}\rrbracket=\left\{[~]\right\}\cup\left\{[x_{i_{0}},\dots,x_{i_{n}}]\in\mathcal{M}_{f}({\llbracket\mathbf{N}\rrbracket})\mid n\in\mathbb{N},\ x_{i_{j}}\!\in\llbracket\underline{i_{j}}\rrbracket\ \forall\,0\leq j\leq n\right\}
.
For the derivation
\underline{n}
in
Example

10
(for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
),
\llbracket\underline{n}\rrbracket=\{([(x_{1},x_{2}),\dots,(x_{n},x_{n+1})],(x_{1},x_{n+1}))\mid x_{1},\dots,x_{n+1}\in D_{X}\}
. Note that
\llbracket\underline{n}\rrbracket\cap\llbracket\underline{m}\rrbracket=\varnothing
for all
n,m\in\mathbb{N}
such that
n\neq m
, and that
\llbracket n\rrbracket
is stable under permutations of the rules
\wn\mathsf{w}
and
\otimes
in
\underline{n}
(that is, if
\mathcal{D}
is obtained from
\underline{n}
by permuting the rules
\wn\mathsf{w}
or
\otimes
, then
\llbracket\mathcal{D}\rrbracket=\llbracket\underline{n}\rrbracket
).

By inspecting the cut-elimination steps and by continuity, we can prove the soundness of relational semantics with respect to cut-elimination (
Theorem

56
), thanks to the fact the interpretation of a coderivation is the union the interpretations of its finite approximation.

Lemma 51
.

Let
\mathcal{D},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\in\mathsf{o}\mathsf{PLL}^{\!\infty}
.

1.

If
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
\mathcal{D}\preceq\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
, then
\llbracket\mathcal{D}\rrbracket_{n}\subseteq\llbracket\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{n}
.

2.

If
\mathcal{D}\preceq\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
then
\llbracket\mathcal{D}\rrbracket\subseteq\llbracket\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\rrbracket
.

Proof.

1.

By induction on
n\in\mathbb{N}
.
By definition,
\llbracket\mathcal{D}\rrbracket_{0}=\varnothing=\llbracket\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{0}
.

Let
n>0
. Let us consider the bottommost rule
\mathsf{r}
in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
.
As
\mathcal{D}\preceq\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
, there are two cases:

•

the bottommost rule in
\mathcal{D}
is
\mathsf{hyp}
and then
\llbracket\mathcal{D}\rrbracket_{n}=\varnothing\subseteq\llbracket\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{n}
;

•

the bottommost rule in
\mathcal{D}
is also
\mathsf{r}
;
thus, if
\mathsf{r}
has no premises then
\mathcal{D}=\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
and hence
\llbracket\mathcal{D}\rrbracket_{n}=\llbracket\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{n}
;
otherwise
\mathcal{D}_{i}\preceq\mathcal{D}_{i}^{\prime}
for every respective premise
\mathcal{D}_{i},\mathcal{D}_{i}^{\prime}
of
\mathsf{r}
in
\mathcal{D},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
, and thus
\llbracket\mathcal{D}\rrbracket_{n}\subseteq\llbracket\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{n}
easily follows from the inductive hypothesis, since
\llbracket\mathcal{D}\rrbracket_{n}
and
\llbracket\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{n}
only depend on
\llbracket\mathcal{D}_{i}\rrbracket_{n-1}
and
\llbracket\mathcal{D}_{i}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{n-1}
, respectively (see
Figure

12
).

2.

According to
Lemma

51
.
1
,
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
\llbracket\mathcal{D}\rrbracket_{n}\subseteq\llbracket\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{n}\subseteq\bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\llbracket\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{n}=\llbracket\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\rrbracket
.
By minimality of the union,
\llbracket\mathcal{D}\rrbracket=\bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\llbracket\mathcal{D}\rrbracket_{n}\subseteq\llbracket\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\rrbracket
.
∎

Definition 52
.

Let
\mathcal{T}
be a tree.
The
height
of a branch of
\mathcal{T}
is the supremum (in
\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
with the expected order) of the heights of its nodes.

A prebar
\mathcal{V}
of a open coderivation labeling a tree
\mathcal{T}
has
uniform height
n\in\mathbb{N}
if all and only the branches of
\mathcal{T}
with height
>n
have a node in
\mathcal{V}
, and all nodes in
\mathcal{V}
have height
n
.

Lemma 53
.

Let
\mathcal{D}\in\mathsf{o}\mathsf{PLL}^{\!\infty}
.
For every
n\in\mathbb{N}
there is
\mathcal{D}_{n}\in\mathcal{K}^{f}({\mathcal{D}})
such that

\llbracket\mathcal{D}_{n}\rrbracket=\llbracket\mathcal{D}\rrbracket_{n}
.

Proof.

By induction on
n\in\mathbb{N}
.
By definition,
\llbracket\mathcal{D}\rrbracket_{0}=\varnothing
.
Let
\mathcal{D}_{0}
the open derivation made only of the rule
\mathsf{hyp}
with the same conclusion as
\mathcal{D}
: then,
\mathcal{D}_{0}\in\mathcal{K}^{f}({\mathcal{D}})
and
\llbracket\mathcal{D}_{0}\rrbracket=\varnothing=\llbracket\mathcal{D}\rrbracket_{0}
(since
\llbracket\mathcal{D}_{0}\rrbracket_{n}=\varnothing
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
).

Let
n>0
. Let
\mathcal{D}_{n}=\lfloor{\mathcal{D}}\rfloor_{\mathcal{V}_{n}}
where
\mathcal{V}_{n}
is a prebar of
\mathcal{D}
with uniform height
n
.
Thus, it is easy to check that
\llbracket\mathcal{D}_{n}\rrbracket_{i}=\llbracket\mathcal{D}\rrbracket_{i}
for every
0\leq i\leq n
, and
\llbracket\mathcal{D}_{n}\rrbracket_{i}=\llbracket\mathcal{D}\rrbracket_{n}
for all
i\geq n
, since
\llbracket\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{0}=\varnothing
for all
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\in\mathsf{o}\mathsf{PLL}^{\!\infty}
and
\llbracket\mathsf{hyp}\rrbracket_{i}=\varnothing
for all
i\in\mathbb{N}
.
∎

Lemma 54
.

Let
\mathcal{D}\in\mathsf{o}\mathsf{PLL}^{\!\infty}
. Then,
\llbracket\mathcal{D}\rrbracket=\llbracket\bigsqcup_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{K}^{f}({\mathcal{D}})}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\rrbracket=\bigcup_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{K}^{f}({\mathcal{D}})}\llbracket\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\rrbracket
.

Proof.

By
Proposition

25
,
\mathcal{D}=\bigsqcup_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{K}^{f}({\mathcal{D}})}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
.

For the left-to-right inclusion, observe that for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
there is
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{n}\in\mathcal{K}^{f}({\mathcal{D}})
such that

\llbracket\bigsqcup_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{K}^{f}({\mathcal{D}})}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{n}=\llbracket\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{n}\rrbracket\subseteq\bigcup_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{K}^{f}({\mathcal{D}})}\llbracket\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\rrbracket
.
Therefore, by minimality of the union,

\llbracket\mathcal{D}\rrbracket=\bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\llbracket\mathcal{D}\rrbracket_{n}=\bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\llbracket\bigsqcup_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{K}^{f}({\mathcal{D}})}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{n}\subseteq\bigcup_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{K}^{f}({\mathcal{D}})}\llbracket\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\rrbracket.

As for the converse inclusion, we have that
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\preceq\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}
implies
\llbracket\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\rrbracket\subseteq\llbracket\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket
. Hence, for all
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{K}^{f}({\mathcal{D}})
, since
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\preceq\bigsqcup_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{K}^{f}({\mathcal{D}})}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}=\mathcal{D}
, we have
\llbracket\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\rrbracket\subseteq\llbracket\mathcal{D}\rrbracket
.
By minimality of the union,
\bigcup_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{K}^{f}({\mathcal{D}})}\llbracket\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\rrbracket\subseteq\llbracket\mathcal{D}\rrbracket
.
∎

Lemma 55
.

Let
\sigma
is a
\mathsf{mc}\text{-}\mathsf{ices}
, and
\mathcal{D}\in\mathsf{o}\mathsf{PLL}^{\!\infty}(\Gamma)
.
If
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{K}^{f}({f_{\sigma}(\mathcal{D})})
then there is
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{K}^{f}({\mathcal{D}})
such that
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}\preceq f_{\sigma}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime})
.

Proof.

As
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{K}^{f}({f_{\sigma}(\mathcal{D})})
, in particular
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}\preceq f_{\sigma}(\mathcal{D})
.
Since
(\mathsf{o}\mathsf{PLL}^{\!\infty}(\Gamma),\preceq)
is a Scott-domain (
Proposition

25
),
\mathcal{D}=\bigsqcup_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{K}^{f}({\mathcal{D}})}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
and hence, by continuity of
f_{\sigma}
(as
\sigma
is a
\mathsf{mc}\text{-}\mathsf{ices}
),
f_{\sigma}(\mathcal{D})=f_{\sigma}(\bigsqcup_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{K}^{f}({\mathcal{D}})}\mathcal{D}^{\prime})=\bigsqcup_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{K}^{f}({\mathcal{D}})}f_{\sigma}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime})
.
By compactness of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}
(
Proposition

25
), from
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}\preceq f_{\sigma}(\mathcal{D})=\bigsqcup_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{K}^{f}({\mathcal{D}})}f_{\sigma}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime})
it follows that
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}\preceq f_{\sigma}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime})
for some
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{K}^{f}({\mathcal{D}})
.
∎

Theorem 56

(Soundness)
.

theoremsoundness

1.

Let
\mathcal{D}\in\mathsf{o}\mathsf{PLL}^{\!\infty}
. If
\mathcal{D}\to_{\mathsf{cut}}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
, then
\llbracket\mathcal{D}\rrbracket=\llbracket\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\rrbracket
.

2.

Let
\mathcal{D}\in\mathsf{o}\mathsf{PLL}^{\!\infty}
. If
\sigma
is a
\mathsf{mc}\text{-}\mathsf{ices}
, then
\llbracket\mathcal{D}\rrbracket=\llbracket f_{\sigma}(\mathcal{D})\rrbracket
.

Proof.

1.

By straightforward inspection of the cut-elimination steps for
\mathsf{o}\mathsf{PLL}^{\!\infty}
.

2.

By definition of
\mathsf{mc}\text{-}\mathsf{ices}
, for any
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{K}^{f}({\mathcal{D}})
we have
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\to_{\mathsf{cut}}^{*}f_{\sigma}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime})
, so
\llbracket\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\rrbracket=\llbracket f_{\sigma}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime})\rrbracket
by
Theorem

56
.
1
.
By
Proposition

25
,
\mathcal{D}=\bigsqcup_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{K}^{f}({\mathcal{D}})}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
.
By continuity of
f_{\sigma}
, we have
f_{\sigma}(\mathcal{D})=\bigsqcup_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{K}^{f}({\mathcal{D}})}f_{\sigma}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime})
.
Thus, using
Lemma

54
in the first equality below,

\llbracket\mathcal{D}\rrbracket=\bigcup_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{K}^{f}({\mathcal{D}})}\llbracket\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\rrbracket=\bigcup_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{K}^{f}({\mathcal{D}})}\llbracket f_{\sigma}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime})\rrbracket=\llbracket\bigsqcup_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{K}^{f}({\mathcal{D}})}f_{\sigma}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime})\rrbracket=\llbracket f_{\sigma}(\mathcal{D})\rrbracket

where the third equality holds for the following reasons:

\subseteq
:

for all
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{K}^{f}({\mathcal{D}})
,
f_{\sigma}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime})\preceq\bigsqcup_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{K}^{f}({\mathcal{D}})}f_{\sigma}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime})
and so
\llbracket f_{\sigma}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime})\rrbracket\subseteq\llbracket\bigsqcup_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{K}^{f}({\mathcal{D}})}f_{\sigma}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime})\rrbracket
by
Lemma

51
;
by minimality of the union,
\bigcup_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{K}^{f}({\mathcal{D}})}\llbracket f_{\sigma}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime})\rrbracket\subseteq\llbracket\bigsqcup_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{K}^{f}({\mathcal{D}})}f_{\sigma}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime})\rrbracket
;

\supseteq
:

according to
Lemma

55
, for all
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{K}^{f}({f_{\sigma}(\mathcal{D})})
, there is
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{K}^{f}({\mathcal{D}})
such that
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}\preceq f_{\sigma}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime})
, and hence
\llbracket\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket\subseteq\llbracket f_{\sigma}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime})\rrbracket\subseteq\bigcup_{D^{\prime}\in\mathcal{K}^{f}({\mathcal{D}})}\llbracket f_{\sigma}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime})\rrbracket
by
Lemma

51
;
by
Lemma

54
and minimality of the union,
\llbracket f_{\sigma}(\mathcal{D})\rrbracket=\bigcup_{D^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{K}^{f}({f_{\sigma}(\mathcal{D})})}\llbracket\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket\subseteq\bigcup_{D^{\prime}\in\mathcal{K}^{f}({\mathcal{D}})}\llbracket f_{\sigma}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime})\rrbracket
.
∎

By
Theorem

56
and since cut-free coderivations have non-empty semantics, we have:

Corollary 57
.

Let
\mathcal{D}\in\mathsf{w}\mathsf{p}\mathsf{PLL}^{\!\infty}
. Then
\llbracket\mathcal{D}\rrbracket\neq\varnothing
.

Proof.

If
\mathcal{D}\in\mathsf{w}\mathsf{p}\mathsf{PLL}^{\!\infty}
is a cut-free coderivation, then weak-progressing ensures the existence of a bar
\mathcal{V}
containing conclusions of rules in
\{\mathsf{ax},\mathsf{1},\mathsf{c\mathsf{\oc p}}\}
. By weak König’s lemma,
\lfloor{\mathcal{D}}\rfloor_{\mathcal{V}}
is finite. Then, we prove by induction on
\lfloor{\mathcal{D}}\rfloor_{\mathcal{V}}
that there is
n\geq 0
such that
\llbracket\lfloor{\mathcal{D}}\rfloor_{\mathcal{V}}\rrbracket_{n}\neq\varnothing
, so that we conclude
\varnothing\neq\llbracket\lfloor{\mathcal{D}}\rfloor_{\mathcal{V}}\rrbracket_{n}\subseteq\llbracket\mathcal{D}\rrbracket_{n}\subseteq\llbracket\mathcal{D}\rrbracket
.
As for the base case, notice that the interpretation of any coderivation ending with the
\mathsf{c\mathsf{\oc p}}
contains the element
(\vec{[~]},[~])
, so it is never empty. The inductive steps are straightforward.

If
\mathcal{D}
contains
\mathsf{cut}
-rules, then

\llbracket\mathcal{D}\rrbracket=\llbracket f_{\sigma}(\mathcal{D})\rrbracket

by
Theorem

56
. Since
f_{\sigma}(\mathcal{D})
is
\mathsf{cut}
-free, we conclude
\llbracket\mathcal{D}\rrbracket\neq\varnothing
by the above reasoning.
∎

We define the set of rules
\mathsf{MELL}^{\!\infty}\coloneqq\mathsf{PLL}^{\!\infty}\cup\{\mathsf{\wn\wn d}\}
where the rule
\mathsf{\wn\wn d}
(
digging
) is defined in
Figure

13
.
We also denote by
\mathsf{MELL}^{\!\infty}
the set of coderivations over the rules in
\mathsf{MELL}^{\!\infty}
.
Relational semantics is naturally extended to
\mathsf{MELL}^{\!\infty}
as shown in
Figure

13
.

The proof system
\mathsf{MELL}^{\!\infty}
can be seen as a non-wellfounded version of
\mathsf{MELL}
.
We show that, as opposed to several fragments of
\mathsf{PLL}^{\!\infty}
, in any good fragment of
\mathsf{MELL}^{\!\infty}
with digging, cut-elimination cannot reduce to cut-free coderivations
and
preserve both the progressing condition and relational semantics.

m\in\mathbb{N}\end{array}\!\!\right\}

Figure 13:
The rule
\mathsf{\wn\wn d}
and its interpretation in the relational semantics (
n>0
).

Theorem 58
.

Let
\mathsf{X}\subseteq\mathsf{MELL}^{\!\infty}
contain non-wellfounded coderivations with
\mathsf{\wn\wn d}
.
Let
\to_{\mathsf{cut}+}
be a cut-elimination relation on
\mathsf{X}
preserving the progressing condition, containing
\to_{\mathsf{cut}}
in
Figures

3
,
5
and
10

and reducing every coderivation in
\mathsf{X}
to a cut-free one.
Then,
\to_{\mathsf{cut}+}
does not preserve relational semantics.

Proof.

Consider the coderivations
\mathcal{D}_{\mathsf{\wn\wn d}}
and
\widehat{\mathcal{D}_{\mathsf{\wn\wn d}}}
below, where

\mathcal{D}=\mathsf{c\mathsf{\oc p}}_{(\underline{0},\underline{1},\underline{0},\underline{1},\ldots)}

and, for all
i\in\mathbb{N}
,
\mathcal{D}_{i}\in\{\mathsf{c\mathsf{\oc p}}_{(\underline{k_{0}^{i}},\dots,\underline{k_{n}^{i}},\ldots)}\mid k_{j}^{i}\in\mathbb{N}\text{ for all }j\in\mathbb{N}\}
(
\underline{n}
is defined in
Example

10
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
).

\mathcal{D}_{\mathsf{\wn\wn d}}\coloneqq{{{{}{}{}}}{}\vbox{\hbox{\kern 3.92212pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{\kern to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$$}}\vbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 14.00002pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\raise-2.39168pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle\mathcal{D}}$}}}}}}}}}}$}}\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$$}\hss}\kern 0.0pt}\kern 1.43518pt\hbox{\kern 14.00002pt\hbox{\hbox{$$}}\kern 14.00002pt}}}\kern 0.0pt}}}\kern 0.0pt\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 8.11115pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$$}}\vbox{}\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$$}\hss}\kern 8.11115pt}\hbox{\kern 8.11115pt\hbox{\hbox{$\oc\mathbf{N}$}}\kern 8.11115pt}}}\kern 0.0pt}\kern 10.00002pt}\hbox{\kern 4.56671pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 9.36938pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\kern 7.26385pt\hbox{}\kern 7.26385pt}\kern 1.43518pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\smash{\lower 2.5pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{ax}\;$}}$}}\vbox{\vbox to0.4pt{\vfill\hbox to42.52774pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt}\vfill}}\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\smash{\lower 2.5pt\hbox{$$}}$}\hss}}\kern 1.43518pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{$\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\wn\wn{\mathbf{N}}^{\bot}\!,\oc\oc\mathbf{N}$}\kern 0.0pt$}}}}}\kern 0.0pt}}}\kern 1.43518pt\hbox{\kern 9.36938pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\smash{\lower 2.5pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{\wn\wn d}\;$}}$}}\vbox{\vbox to0.4pt{\vfill\hbox to42.52774pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt}\vfill}}\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\smash{\lower 2.5pt\hbox{$$}}$}\hss}\kern 0.0pt}\kern 1.43518pt\hbox{\kern 11.7305pt\hbox{\hbox{$\kern 0.0pt\hbox{${\wn{\mathbf{N}}^{\bot}\!,\oc\oc\mathbf{N}}$}\kern 0.0pt$}}\kern 2.36111pt}}}\kern 0.0pt}}}}\kern 1.43518pt\hbox{\kern 8.11115pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\smash{\lower 2.5pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{cut}\;$}}$}}\vbox{\vbox to0.4pt{\vfill\hbox to83.99162pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt}\vfill}}\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\smash{\lower 2.5pt\hbox{$$}}$}\hss}\kern 2.36111pt}\kern 1.43518pt\hbox{\kern 42.8292pt\hbox{\hbox{$\oc\oc\mathbf{N}$}}\kern 37.07916pt}}}\kern 0.0pt}}}\qquad\qquad\widehat{\mathcal{D}_{\mathsf{\wn\wn d}}}\coloneqq{{{{}{}{}}}{{{{}{}{}}}{{{{}{}{}}}{}}}\vbox{\hbox{\kern 3.65825pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{$$}}\kern 10.00002pt}\hbox{\hbox{$\hskip 8.0pt$}}\kern 10.00002pt}\hbox{\hbox{$$}}}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$$}}\vbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 14.00002pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.89168pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle\mathcal{D}_{1}}$}}}}}}}}}}$}}\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$$}\hss}\kern 0.0pt}\kern 1.43518pt\hbox{\kern 14.00002pt\hbox{\hbox{$$}}\kern 14.00002pt}}}\kern 0.0pt}}}\kern 0.0pt\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 8.11115pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$$}}\vbox{}\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$$}\hss}\kern 8.11115pt}\hbox{\kern 8.11115pt\hbox{\hbox{$\oc\mathbf{N}$}}\kern to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$$}}\vbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 14.00002pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}{}}{{}{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern-5.46242pt\raise 7.64386pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.89168pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle\mathcal{D}_{n}}$}}}}}}}}}}$}}\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$$}\hss}\kern 0.0pt}\kern 1.43518pt\hbox{\kern 14.00002pt\hbox{\hbox{$$}}\kern 14.00002pt}}}\kern 0.0pt}}}\kern 0.0pt\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 8.11115pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$$}}\vbox{}\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$$}\hss}\kern 8.11115pt}\hbox{\kern 8.11115pt\hbox{\hbox{$\oc\mathbf{N}$}}\kern 8.11115pt}}}\kern 0.0pt}\kern 10.00002pt}\hbox{\kern 11.7694pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\kern 7.27776pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{$\vdots$}}}\kern 7.27776pt}\kern 1.43518pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\smash{\lower 2.5pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c\mathsf{\oc p}}\;$}}$}}\vbox{\vbox to0.4pt{\vfill\hbox to14.55553pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt}\vfill}}\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\smash{\lower 2.5pt\hbox{$$}}$}\hss}}\kern 1.43518pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{$\kern 0.0pt\hbox{${\oc\oc\mathbf{N}}$}\kern 0.0pt$}}}}}\kern 0.0pt}}}}\kern 1.43518pt\hbox{\kern 8.11115pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\smash{\lower 2.5pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c\mathsf{\oc p}}\;$}}$}}\vbox{\vbox to0.4pt{\vfill\hbox to56.21382pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt}\vfill}}\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\smash{\lower 2.5pt\hbox{$$}}$}\hss}\kern 0.0pt}\kern 1.43518pt\hbox{\kern 32.46805pt\hbox{\hbox{$\reflectbox{$\ddots$}$}}\kern 24.3569pt}}}\kern 0.0pt}}}}\kern 1.43518pt\hbox{\kern 8.11115pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\smash{\lower 2.5pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c\mathsf{\oc p}}\;$}}$}}\vbox{\vbox to0.4pt{\vfill\hbox to73.5152pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt}\vfill}}\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\smash{\lower 2.5pt\hbox{$$}}$}\hss}\kern 24.3569pt}\kern 1.43518pt\hbox{\kern 37.59097pt\hbox{\hbox{${\oc\oc\mathbf{N}}$}}\kern 53.83673pt}}}\kern 0.0pt}}}}\kern 1.43518pt\hbox{\kern 8.11115pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\smash{\lower 2.5pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c\mathsf{\oc p}}\;$}}$}}\vbox{\vbox to0.4pt{\vfill\hbox to85.69365pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt}\vfill}}\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\smash{\lower 2.5pt\hbox{$$}}$}\hss}\kern 53.83673pt}\kern 1.43518pt\hbox{\kern 43.6802pt\hbox{\hbox{$\oc\oc\mathbf{N}$}}\kern 89.40579pt}}}\kern 0.0pt}}}

Coderivations
\widehat{\mathcal{D}_{\mathsf{\wn\wn d}}}
are the only
\mathsf{cut}
-free and progressing ones with conclusion
\oc\oc\mathbf{N}
.
Indeed, any
\mathsf{cut}
-free coderivation of
\oc\oc\mathbf{N}
or
\oc\mathbf{N}
must end with a
\mathsf{c\mathsf{\oc p}}
, and the only
\mathsf{cut}
-free and progressing coderivations of
\mathbf{N}
are the derivations of the form
\underline{n}
for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
, up to permutations of the rules
\wn\mathsf{w}
and
\mathop{\otimes}
(other
\mathsf{cut}
-free coderivations of
\mathbf{N}
exist, but they have an infinite branch containing infinitely many
\wn\mathsf{b}
rules and no
\mathsf{c\mathsf{\oc p}}
rules, hence they are not progressing).
Therefore, for whatever definition of the cut-elimination steps concerning
\mathsf{\wn\wn d}
that preserves the progressing condition, necessarily
\mathcal{D}_{\mathsf{\wn\wn d}}
will reduce to
\widehat{\mathcal{D}_{\mathsf{\wn\wn d}}}
, since
\mathcal{D}_{\mathsf{\wn\wn d}}
is progressing.

We show that
\llbracket\widehat{\mathcal{D}_{\mathsf{\wn\wn d}}}\rrbracket\not\subseteq\llbracket\mathcal{D}_{\mathsf{\wn\wn d}}\rrbracket
.
First, it can be easily shown that if, in one of the
\mathcal{D}_{i}=\mathsf{c\mathsf{\oc p}}_{(\underline{k_{0}^{i}},\dots,\underline{k_{n}^{i}},\ldots)}
in
\widehat{\mathcal{D}_{\mathsf{\wn\wn d}}}
, one of the
k_{j}^{i}
is different from
0
or
1
, then there is
x\in\llbracket\widehat{\mathcal{D}_{\mathsf{\wn\wn d}}}\rrbracket\smallsetminus\llbracket\mathcal{D}_{\mathsf{\wn\wn d}}\rrbracket
(this basically follows from the fact that
\llbracket\underline{n}\rrbracket\cap\llbracket\underline{m}\rrbracket=\varnothing
for all
n,m\in\mathbb{N}
such that
n\neq m
, see
Example

50
).
Let us now suppose that in
\widehat{\mathcal{D}_{\mathsf{\wn\wn d}}}
, for all
i\in\mathbb{N}
,
\mathcal{D}_{i}=\mathsf{c\mathsf{\oc p}}_{(\underline{k_{0}^{i}},\dots,\underline{k_{n}^{i}},\ldots)}
with
k_{j}^{i}\in\{0,1\}
for all
j\in\mathbb{N}
.
Let
\hat{0}
and
\hat{1}
be any element of
\llbracket\underline{0}\rrbracket
and
\llbracket\underline{1}\rrbracket
, respectively (see
Example

50
).
Note that
\hat{0}\neq\hat{1}
.
It is easy to verify that
[[\hat{0}],[\hat{0}]],[[\hat{1}],[\hat{1}]]\notin\llbracket\mathcal{D}_{\mathsf{\wn\wn d}}\rrbracket
,
since
[\hat{0},\hat{0}],[\hat{1},\hat{1}]\notin\llbracket\mathcal{D}\rrbracket
(see
Example

50
).
Concerning
\llbracket\widehat{\mathcal{D}_{\mathsf{\wn\wn d}}}\rrbracket
, notice that, since
k^{0}_{0},k^{1}_{0},k^{2}_{0}\in\{0,1\}
, either
k^{0}_{0}=k^{1}_{0}
or
k^{1}_{0}=k^{2}_{0}
or
k^{2}_{0}=k^{0}_{0}
. In the first case, we have
[[k^{0}_{0}],[k^{1}_{0}]]\in\llbracket\widehat{\mathcal{D}_{\mathsf{\wn\wn d}}}\rrbracket
, in the second case we have
[[k^{1}_{0}],[k^{2}_{0}]]\in\llbracket\widehat{\mathcal{D}_{\mathsf{\wn\wn d}}}\rrbracket
, and in the last case we have
[[k^{2}_{0}],[k^{0}_{0}]]\in\llbracket\widehat{\mathcal{D}_{\mathsf{\wn\wn d}}}\rrbracket
.
∎

7
Conclusion and future work

For future research, we envisage extending our contributions in many directions. First, our notion of finite approximation seems intimately related with that of Taylor expansion from
differential linear logic
(
\mathsf{DiLL}
)
[
15
]
, where the rule
\mathsf{hyp}
(quite like the rule
0
from
\mathsf{DiLL}
) serves to model approximations of
boxes
. This connection with Taylor expansions becomes even more apparent in Mazza’s original systems for parsimonious logic
[
25
,
26
]
, which comprise co-absorption and co-weakening rules typical of
\mathsf{DiLL}
. These considerations deserve further investigations. Secondly, building on a series of recent works in
Cyclic Implicit Complexity
, i.e., implicit computational complexity in the setting of circular and non-wellfounded proof theory
[
10
,
9
]
, we are currently working on second-order extensions of
\mathsf{wr}\mathsf{PLL}^{\!\infty}
and
\mathsf{r}\mathsf{PLL}^{\!\infty}
to characterize the complexity classes
\mathbf{P}/\mathsf{poly}
and
\mathbf{P}
(see
[
23
]
). These results would reformulate in a non-wellfounded setting the characterization of
\mathbf{P}/\mathsf{poly}
presented in
[
26
]
.

Appendix of
Section

3

Proof.

We recall the sequent calculus for (propositional)
multiplicative exponential linear logic

\mathsf{MELL}=\{\mathsf{ax},\mathop{\otimes},\invamp,\mathsf{1},\bot,\mathsf{cut},\mathsf{\oc p},\wn\mathsf{w},\mathsf{\wn d},\wn\mathsf{c}\}

where
the
promotion
(
\mathsf{\oc p}
),
dereliction
(
\mathsf{\wn d}
),
contraction
(
\wn\mathsf{c}
) rules are defined as follows:

\wn\Gamma,A

\scriptstyle\mathsf{\oc p}\;

\wn\Gamma,\oc A

\Gamma,A

\scriptstyle\mathsf{\wn d}\;

\Gamma,\wn A

\Gamma,\wn A,\wn A

\scriptstyle\wn\mathsf{c}\;

\Gamma,\wn A

(7)

We also denote by
\mathsf{MELL}
the set of derivations over the rules in
\mathsf{MELL}
,
and we map each derivation in
\mathcal{D}\in\mathsf{PLL}
to
a derivation in
\left(\mathcal{D}\right)^{\spadesuit}\in\mathsf{MELL}

\left(\cdot\right)^{\spadesuit}\colon\mathsf{PLL}\to\mathsf{MELL}

defined in
Figure

14
by induction on derivations.

In order to prove that the following diagram commute,

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}^{\spadesuit}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\right)^{\spadesuit}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\spadesuit}
possibly many steps
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\spadesuit}

Each cut-elimination step in
\mathsf{PLL}
corresponds to a cut-elimination step in
\mathsf{MELL}
except the ones in
Figures

15
and
16
,
where a cut-elimination step in
\mathsf{PLL}
can be simulated by a sequence of cut-elimination steps in
\mathsf{MELL}
.
In these Figures each macro-step denoted by
\mathrel{\mathrlap{\rightarrow}\mkern 0.1mu\rightarrow}

involves a unique step from
Figures

4
and
5
(the one marked) and certain additional commutative cut-elimination steps of the following form below

\footnotesize{{}{}\vbox{\hbox{\kern 9.62666pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{$\Gamma,A\!\!\!\!\!$}}\kern 8.50012pt}\hbox{\kern 8.73227pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\kern to33.75592pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt}\vfill}}\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\smash{\lower 2.12503pt\hbox{$$}}$}\hss}\kern 5.29173pt}\kern 1.14815pt\hbox{\kern 13.61955pt\hbox{\hbox{$\kern (8)

which push
\mathsf{\wn d}
down a cut and create an alternating chain of
\mathsf{\wn d}
and
\wn\mathsf{c}
(such additional steps are natural to consider since they involve rule permutations of independent rules and would appear whenever a
\mathsf{cut}
-rule would interact with the
\wn
-formula introduced by the
\mathsf{\wn d}
-rule).
Thus, the derivation in
\mathsf{MELL}
obtained by (standard and additional) cut-elimination from
\mathcal{D}^{\spadesuit}
is exactly the translation
\left(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\right)^{\spadesuit}
of the derivation
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
in
\mathsf{PLL}
obtained after a cut-elimination step from
\mathcal{D}
.
According to the definition of
\left(\cdot\right)^{\spadesuit}
, if
\left(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\right)^{\spadesuit}
is cut-free then so is
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
.

The termination of cut-elimination in
\mathsf{MELL}
with this additional commutative step follows from the result in
\mathsf{MELL}

[
29
]
.
Indeed, to the usual measure
m
that decreases after each standard cut-elimination step in
\mathsf{MELL}
(and remains unchanged after each additional step in (
8
)), we can add the sum
d
of the heights of the
\mathsf{\wn d}
rules in a derivation, which decreases after each step in (
8
). Thus, the measure
(m,d)
with the lexicographical order decreases after each (standard or additional) cut-elimination step in
\mathsf{MELL}
.

∎
